regularization is functorial for conjugacy transport, because it is expressed as

\operatorname{Ind}_{G^{\circ}}^{G}\rho^{\circ}
and induction across the normal inclusion

G^{\circ}\lhd G
commutes with such transport. Therefore any finite stable
transport preserving the connected coefficient also preserves the enriched
regularized coefficient. This is the finite stability property used in the
local depth-zero application.
∎

14.
Finite-field preservation of rank-one Hecke parameters

In this section, we reprove
[
24
, Proposition 3.2.3]
by reducing the comparison of
q
-parameters to a comparison of Schur elements arising from the endomorphism algebras of Harish–Chandra induced representations.

Let
\mathbf{G}
be a connected reductive group over a finite field
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, and let
\mathbf{L}\subseteq\mathbf{G}
be an
F
-stable Levi subgroup of an
F
-stable parabolic subgroup of
\mathbf{G}
.
Let
\tau
be an irreducible cuspidal representation of
L
, and assume
\tau\in\mathcal{E}(L,s)
for a semisimple element
s\in L^{*}
, where
\mathbf{L}^{*}
is the dual group of
\mathbf{L}
and
\mathbf{L}^{*}\subset\mathbf{G}^{*}
is an
F^{*}
-stable Levi subgroup of an
F^{*}
-stable parabolic subgroup of
\mathbf{G}^{*}
.

Let
u_{\tau}\in\operatorname{Uch}(H_{L})
denote the unipotent representation of the centralizer
H_{L}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=C_{L^{*}}(s)
associated with
\tau
through the Jordan decomposition of characters
\mathbb{J}_{s}^{L}
as in Theorem
11.4
.
Consider the Harish-Chandra inductions:

\pi\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=R_{\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{G}}(\tau),\qquad\pi^{*}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=R_{\mathbf{H}_{L}}^{\mathbf{H}}(u_{\tau}).

Assume that
R_{\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{G}}(\tau)
has length at most two. If

R_{\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{G}}(\tau)=\pi_{1}\oplus\pi_{2}

has two irreducible constituents, ordered so that

\operatorname{dim}\pi_{1}\geq\operatorname{dim}\pi_{2}
, set

q\bigl(R_{\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{G}}(\tau)\bigr)=\frac{\operatorname{dim}\pi_{1}}{\operatorname{dim}\pi_{2}}.

If
R_{\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{G}}(\tau)
is irreducible, set

q\bigl(R_{\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{G}}(\tau)\bigr)=1.

We aim to prove the following.

q(\pi)=q(\pi^{*}),

14.1.
Schur element

Associated with the cuspidal pair
(L,\tau)
is the relative Hecke algebra

\mathcal{H}_{G}(M,\tau)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\operatorname{End}_{G}(R_{\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{G}}(\tau)),

which is a finite type symmetric algebra over a ring
A=\mathbb{Z}[\mathbf{x}^{\pm 1}]
of Hecke parameters.
Let
W_{\tau}
denote the relative Weyl group associated with the pair
(L,\tau)
,
and let
K
be a splitting field for
\mathcal{H}_{G}(L,\tau)
.

By
[
11
, Theorem 3.2.5]
, there is a well-known bijection:

\mathrm{Irr}(\mathcal{H}_{G}(L,\tau))\longleftrightarrow\operatorname{Irr}(G,(L,\tau)),

where each irreducible character
\phi\in\operatorname{Irr}(W_{\tau})
corresponds to a unique irreducible constituent
\pi_{\phi}
of
\pi=R_{\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{G}}(\tau)
.
Let
c_{\phi}\in A^{\prime}
denote the
Schur element
associated with
\phi
with respect to the canonical symmetrizing form
\lambda
on
\mathcal{H}_{G}(L,\tau)
, where
A^{\prime}
denotes the integral closure of
A
in
K
. Then the character of
\lambda
decomposes as:

\lambda=\sum_{\phi\in\operatorname{Irr}(W_{\tau})}\frac{1}{c_{\phi}}\chi_{\phi},

where
\chi_{\phi}
is the character of the simple module corresponding to
\phi
.
Upon specialization
\mathbf{x}\mapsto 1
, the Schur elements
c_{\phi}(q)
become rational numbers.
By
[
11
, Theorem 3.2.18]
, the dimension of the irreducible representation
\pi_{\phi}\in\operatorname{Irr}(G,(L,\tau))
corresponding to the irreducible representation
\phi\in\operatorname{Irr}(W_{\tau})
is given by:

\operatorname{dim}(\pi_{\phi})=\operatorname{dim}(\pi)\cdot c_{\phi}(q)^{-1}.

In particular, if
\pi=\pi_{1}\oplus\pi_{2}
corresponds to the characters
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\in\operatorname{Irr}(W_{\tau})
, then:

q(\pi)=\frac{\operatorname{dim}(\pi_{1})}{\operatorname{dim}(\pi_{2})}=\frac{c_{\phi_{2}}(q)}{c_{\phi_{1}}(q)}.

14.2.
Compatibility Under Jordan Decomposition of characters

By Theorem
11.4
, the Jordan decomposition of characters can be chosen to commute with Harish-Chandra induction.
Moreover, for any Levi subgroup
\mathbf{L}\subseteq\mathbf{G}
as before, and any
\tau\in\mathcal{E}(L,s)
, we have a canonical isomorphism of relative Hecke algebras:

\mathcal{H}_{G}(L,\tau)\cong\mathcal{H}_{H}(H_{L},u_{\tau}),

which preserves the indexing of irreducible characters and the associated Schur elements.
Therefore, the constituents
u_{1},u_{2}
of
\pi^{*}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=R_{\mathbf{H}_{L}}^{\mathbf{H}}(u_{\tau})
correspond to the same
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\in\operatorname{Irr}(W_{\tau})=\operatorname{Irr}(W_{u_{\tau}})
, and satisfy:

\operatorname{dim}(u_{i})=\operatorname{dim}(\pi^{*})\cdot c_{\phi_{i}}(q)^{-1},\quad\text{for }i=1,2.

Consequently,

q(\pi^{*})=\frac{\operatorname{dim}(u_{1})}{\operatorname{dim}(u_{2})}=\frac{c_{\phi_{2}}(q)}{c_{\phi_{1}}(q)}=q(\pi).

As a consequence, we get that

Theorem 14.1

(Preservation of rank-one finite-field parameters under Jordan decomposition)
.

With notation as above, assume that
R_{\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{G}}(\tau)
has length at most two.
Then
R_{\mathbf{H}_{L}}^{\mathbf{H}}(u_{\tau})
also has length at most two, and

q\left(R_{\mathbf{L}}^{\mathbf{G}}(\tau)\right)=q\left(R_{\mathbf{H}_{L}}^{\mathbf{H}}(u_{\tau})\right).

15.
Application: depth-zero to unipotent affine Hecke algebra factors

In this section we explain how the finite-field comparison proved above gives a
pinned canonical form of Ohara’s comparison between depth-zero affine Hecke
algebra parameters and unipotent affine Hecke algebra parameters. The conclusion
is deliberately restricted to the affine Hecke algebra factor. The full
AFMO–Morris Hecke algebra usually has the form of an affine Hecke algebra
crossed with a finite twisted group algebra, and Ohara’s theorem does not identify
that finite crossed-product factor with the corresponding finite crossed-product
factor for the unipotent type.

Thus the result below should be read as a canonical version of the affine-factor
comparison in
[
24
, Theorem 4.4.1]
. It does not assert a general
isomorphism from a depth-zero Hecke algebra to the Hecke algebra of a unipotent
type. Instead, it says that, after fixing the pinning, the affine Hecke algebra
factor of the depth-zero algebra may be replaced by the corresponding unipotent
affine Hecke algebra factor, while the finite group
\Omega(\rho_{M})
and the
cocycle
\mu
in the crossed product remain the original source-side ones.

The only additional point which must be made explicit for our use of the finite
Jordan decomposition is that the finite groups occurring in the depth-zero
construction are not always the connected reductive quotients

G(F)_{x,0}/G(F)_{x,0+}
. They are often the full quotients

G(F)_{x}/G(F)_{x,0+}
, and these may be disconnected. We therefore first record
why these full quotients satisfy the hypotheses imposed in the enriched
disconnected Jordan decomposition theorem.

15.1.
Full parahoric quotients and the pinned-component condition

Let
F
be a non-archimedean local field with residue field
\mathfrak{f}
, and let

G
be a connected reductive group over
F
. For a vertex

x\in\mathcal{B}(G,F)
, put

K_{x}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=G(F)_{x},\qquad K_{x}^{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=G(F)_{x,0},\qquad K_{x}^{+}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=G(F)_{x,0+}.

We write

\overline{G}_{x}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=K_{x}/K_{x}^{+},\qquad\overline{G}_{x}^{\circ}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=K_{x}^{0}/K_{x}^{+}.

Thus
\overline{G}_{x}^{\circ}
is the group of
\mathfrak{f}
-points of the connected
reductive quotient of the parahoric group scheme attached to
K_{x}^{0}
. To avoid
confusing this finite group with an integral model, we denote that connected
reductive
\mathfrak{f}
-group by

\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}^{\circ},\qquad\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{f})=\overline{G}_{x}^{\circ}.

The full quotient
\overline{G}_{x}
will be regarded as the group of
\mathfrak{f}
-points
of the corresponding possibly disconnected reductive quotient

\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}
, with identity component

\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}^{\circ}
.

Fix the global pinned datum
\mathcal{P}
used throughout the paper. For each
vertex
x
occurring below, we denote by

\mathcal{P}_{x}^{\mathcal{P}}

the
\mathfrak{f}
-pinning of
\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}^{\circ}
induced by the global
pinning
\mathcal{P}
. Concretely, the construction may be described by taking the

\mathcal{P}
-compatible alcove adjacent to
x
, using it to determine the Borel
subgroup of
\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}^{\circ}
, and reducing the root-group
parametrizations of
\mathcal{P}
through the Bruhat–Tits root-group quotients at

x
. We shall sometimes denote this local alcove by
\mathfrak{a}
inside
proofs, but
\mathfrak{a}
is not an additional datum: the finite-quotient
pinning used below is
\mathcal{P}_{x}^{\mathcal{P}}
, determined by the fixed global pinning

\mathcal{P}
.

Lemma 15.1

(Component group of the full parahoric quotient)
.

With notation as above, the component group of the full quotient is abelian. More
precisely, the Kottwitz homomorphism induces an injective homomorphism

K_{x}/K_{x}^{0}\hookrightarrow X^{*}(Z(\widehat{G})^{I})^{\Phi}.

Consequently

\overline{G}_{x}/\overline{G}_{x}^{\circ}\cong K_{x}/K_{x}^{0}

is abelian.

Proof.

The parahoric subgroup
K_{x}^{0}
is the kernel, inside the stabilizer
K_{x}
, of
the Kottwitz invariant. Equivalently, the Kottwitz map is trivial on

G(F)_{x,0}
and separates the components of the stabilizer of the vertex

x
. Hence it induces the displayed injection

K_{x}/K_{x}^{0}\hookrightarrow X^{*}(Z(\widehat{G})^{I})^{\Phi}.

The last assertion follows because

\overline{G}_{x}/\overline{G}_{x}^{\circ}=(K_{x}/K_{x}^{+})/(K_{x}^{0}/K_{x}^{+})\cong K_{x}/K_{x}^{0}.

∎

Lemma 15.2

(Parahoric component actions are pinned modulo the connected quotient)
.

Let
\bar{k}\in\overline{G}_{x}
, and let

\alpha_{\bar{k}}\in\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathfrak{f}}(\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}^{\circ})

be the automorphism induced by conjugation on

\overline{G}_{x}^{\circ}=K_{x}^{0}/K_{x}^{+}
. Then there exist

\bar{g}_{\bar{k}}\in\overline{G}_{x}^{\circ}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sigma_{\bar{k}}\in\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathfrak{f}}(\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}^{\circ},\mathcal{P}_{x}^{\mathcal{P}})

such that

\alpha_{\bar{k}}=\operatorname{Int}(\bar{g}_{\bar{k}})\circ\sigma_{\bar{k}}.

Equivalently, if

\overline{G}_{x,\mathcal{P}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{\bar{k}\in\overline{G}_{x}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\operatorname{Ad}(\bar{k})(\mathcal{P}_{x}^{\mathcal{P}})=\mathcal{P}_{x}^{\mathcal{P}}\},

then

\overline{G}_{x}=\overline{G}_{x}^{\circ}\cdot\overline{G}_{x,\mathcal{P}}.

In particular, the possibly disconnected finite reductive quotient

\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}
, together with the pinning

\mathcal{P}_{x}^{\mathcal{P}}
of its identity component, satisfies
Hypothesis
12.1
.

Proof.

We recall the standard normalizer description of parahoric stabilizers. Let

\mathfrak{a}=\mathfrak{a}_{\mathcal{P},x}
denote the
\mathcal{P}
-compatible local alcove
used to describe the induced pinning
\mathcal{P}_{x}^{\mathcal{P}}
. Choose a maximal

F
-split torus
S
whose apartment contains
x
and this alcove. Let

N=N_{G}(S)(F),\qquad\widetilde{W}=N/S(F)_{0}

be the Iwahori–Weyl group. The stabilizer
K_{x}
is generated by
K_{x}^{0}
and
the stabilizer of
x
in
N
. Hence every class in

K_{x}/K_{x}^{0}
has a representative
n\in N\cap K_{x}
.

Let
k\in K_{x}
be a lift of
\bar{k}
, and choose such an
n
in the same
class modulo
K_{x}^{0}
. Then
kn^{-1}\in K_{x}^{0}
. If
\bar{h}
denotes the
image of
kn^{-1}
in
\overline{G}_{x}^{\circ}
, then on

\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}^{\circ}

\alpha_{\bar{k}}=\operatorname{Int}(\bar{h})\circ\alpha_{\bar{n}},

where
\bar{n}
is the image of
n
in
\overline{G}_{x}
. It is therefore
enough to treat representatives coming from
N\cap K_{x}
.

For such an
n
, conjugation by
n
stabilizes
x
and sends the alcove

\mathfrak{a}
to another alcove whose closure contains
x
. On the reductive
quotient, it sends the Borel, torus, and simple root-group quotients defining

\mathcal{P}_{x}^{\mathcal{P}}
to the corresponding pinned data attached to

n\mathfrak{a}
, with the root-group parametrizations transported by the fixed
Tits normalizations. The two alcoves
\mathfrak{a}
and
n\mathfrak{a}

determine two
\mathfrak{f}
-rational pinnings of the connected reductive quotient

\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}^{\circ}
arising from the same Bruhat–Tits root-group
parametrizations. The two chamber descriptions
\mathfrak{a}
and
n\mathfrak{a}
determine
two normalized pinnings of
\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}^{\circ}
, both obtained
from the fixed global pinning
\mathcal{P}
by the Bruhat–Tits root-group
filtrations and the Tits normalizations. In the finite BN-pair of

\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}^{\circ}(\mathfrak{f})
, the chambers corresponding to

\mathfrak{a}
and
n\mathfrak{a}
differ by an element of the finite Weyl
group. Choosing the corresponding Tits representative gives an element

\bar{b}_{n}\in\overline{G}_{x}^{\circ}
which carries this induced normalized
pinning attached to
n\mathfrak{a}
back to the induced normalized pinning
attached to
\mathfrak{a}
.
Equivalently,

\operatorname{Int}(\bar{b}_{n})\circ\alpha_{\bar{n}}\in\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathfrak{f}}(\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}^{\circ},\mathcal{P}_{x}^{\mathcal{P}}).

Set

\sigma_{\bar{n}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\operatorname{Int}(\bar{b}_{n})\circ\alpha_{\bar{n}}.

Then

\alpha_{\bar{n}}=\operatorname{Int}(\bar{b}_{n}^{-1})\circ\sigma_{\bar{n}}.

Combining this with the previous decomposition of
\alpha_{\bar{k}}
, we obtain

\alpha_{\bar{k}}=\operatorname{Int}(\bar{h}\bar{b}_{n}^{-1})\circ\sigma_{\bar{n}}.

This is the required formula, with

\bar{g}_{\bar{k}}=\bar{h}\bar{b}_{n}^{-1}
and

\sigma_{\bar{k}}=\sigma_{\bar{n}}
.

The equivalent factorization of
\overline{G}_{x}
follows immediately: if

\alpha_{\bar{k}}=\operatorname{Int}(\bar{g}_{\bar{k}})\circ\sigma_{\bar{k}}
, then

\bar{g}_{\bar{k}}^{-1}\bar{k}
acts on

\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}^{\circ}
by the pinning-preserving automorphism

\sigma_{\bar{k}}
. Hence

\bar{g}_{\bar{k}}^{-1}\bar{k}\in\overline{G}_{x,\mathcal{P}}
, and so

\bar{k}\in\overline{G}_{x}^{\circ}\cdot\overline{G}_{x,\mathcal{P}}
. The reverse inclusion
is tautological. This proves the rational pinned-component condition for the
full quotient.
∎

Remark 15.3
.

In the application below, the enriched disconnected Jordan decomposition theorem
is used only to make the finite Jordan-decomposition input canonical for the
full finite quotient
\overline{G}_{x}=G(F)_{x}/G(F)_{x,0+}
. The connected quotient

\underline{\mathbf{G}}_{x}^{\circ}
enters because the pinning is a pinning of the
identity component, as in Hypothesis
12.1
.
Lemmas
15.1
and

15.2
verify precisely the two additional
inputs needed to use Theorem
13.4
for these full parahoric
quotients. This use of the disconnected theorem concerns the finite
Jordan-decomposition labels and the finite-field parameter comparison; it does
not identify the finite crossed-product factor in the AFMO Hecke algebra with a
unipotent crossed-product factor.

15.2.
Set-up and statement

Let
F
be a non-archimedean local field with residue field
\mathfrak{f}
of
characteristic
p
, and fix a coefficient field
\mathbb{C}
of characteristic

0
. Let
G
be a connected reductive group defined over
F
. We assume
the standard tameness hypotheses under which the depth-zero type theory of

[
2
,
1
]
applies, as recalled in
[
24
, §1]
:

(15.1)

G\text{ splits over a tamely ramified extension of }F,\qquad p\nmid|W_{\mathrm{abs}}(G)|.

Fix once and for all a global pinned datum
\mathcal{P}
for
G
in the sense used
above. Whenever a parahoric quotient occurs in the construction, we use the
induced pinning
\mathcal{P}_{x}^{\mathcal{P}}
on its connected reductive quotient.

Let
\mathfrak{s}=[M,\sigma]_{G}
be a depth-zero inertial equivalence class for

G(F)
. Let
(K_{x_{0}},\rho_{x_{0}})
be the depth-zero
\mathfrak{s}
-type of

[
2
]
, as recalled in
[
24
, §4.2]
, and denote its Hecke
algebra by

\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{s}}(G)\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\mathcal{H}\bigl(G(F),(K_{x_{0}},\rho_{x_{0}})\bigr).

By
[
24
, Theorem 4.2.2]
, equivalently by

[
2
, Theorem 5.3.6]
, this algebra has the AFMO–Morris presentation

(15.2)

\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{s}}(G)\simeq\mathbb{C}[\Omega(\rho_{M}),\mu]\rtimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}\!\bigl(W(\rho_{M})_{\mathrm{aff}},q\bigr).

Here
W(\rho_{M})_{\mathrm{aff}}
is the affine Weyl group attached to the
depth-zero datum,
q
is the corresponding parameter function,

\Omega(\rho_{M})
is the stabilizer of the distinguished chamber in the relevant
extended symmetry group, and
\mu
is the AFMO
2
-cocycle appearing in the
twisted group algebra.

From
(K_{x_{0}},\rho_{x_{0}})
, Ohara constructs in
[
24
, §4.3]
a
connected reductive group
G_{\theta}
splitting over an unramified extension of

F
, together with a unipotent type
(K_{\theta,x_{0}},u_{x_{0}})
for

G_{\theta}(F)
. Ohara’s corresponding unipotent type has Hecke algebra

(15.3)

\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{unip}}(G_{\theta})\simeq\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}\!\bigl(W(u_{\rho_{M}})_{\mathrm{aff}},q_{\theta}\bigr),

as in
[
24
, Theorem 4.3.7]
. In particular, the unipotent
Hecke algebra appearing in Ohara’s comparison is the affine Hecke algebra
factor; it is not enlarged by the source-side twisted group algebra.

Theorem 15.4

(Pinned comparison of affine Hecke algebra factors)
.

Assume (
15.1
). With notation as above, the fixed pinning
\mathcal{P}

canonically determines an isomorphism of affine Hecke algebras

\Psi_{\mathcal{P}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}\!\bigl(W(\rho_{M})_{\mathrm{aff}},q\bigr)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}\!\bigl(W(u_{\rho_{M}})_{\mathrm{aff}},q_{\theta}\bigr).

Equivalently, using the AFMO–Morris presentation of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{s}}(G)
, one
may rewrite the depth-zero Hecke algebra as

\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{s}}(G)\simeq\mathbb{C}[\Omega(\rho_{M}),\mu]\rtimes_{\Psi_{\mathcal{P}}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}\!\bigl(W(u_{\rho_{M}})_{\mathrm{aff}},q_{\theta}\bigr),

where the finite group
\Omega(\rho_{M})
, the cocycle
\mu
, and the crossed-product
structure are the original source-side ones, with the action transported across

\Psi_{\mathcal{P}}
. This statement does not assert, and in general should not be
read as asserting, an isomorphism

\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{s}}(G)\cong\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{unip}}(G_{\theta}).

The affine-factor isomorphism preserves the standard anti-involutions on the two
affine Hecke algebra factors.

15.3.
Proof

Proof.

The two crossed-product presentations (
15.2
) and
(
15.3
) show where the comparison can be made. Ohara’s
comparison theorem
[
24
, Theorem 4.4.1]
identifies the affine
reflection data on the two sides:

H_{\mathcal{K}\text{-rel}}=H_{\mathcal{K}_{\theta}\text{-rel}},\qquad W(\rho_{M})_{\mathrm{aff}}\cong W(u_{\rho_{M}})_{\mathrm{aff}},

and the parameter functions agree under this identification,

q=q_{\theta}.

Consequently one obtains an isomorphism of affine Hecke algebra factors

(15.4)

\Psi_{\mathcal{P}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}\!\bigl(W(\rho_{M})_{\mathrm{aff}},q\bigr)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}\!\bigl(W(u_{\rho_{M}})_{\mathrm{aff}},q_{\theta}\bigr),

characterized by sending the standard generator attached to a simple reflection
to the standard generator attached to the corresponding simple reflection.

We now explain the role of the pinned Jordan decomposition in this comparison.
The proof of
[
24
, Theorem 4.4.1]
reduces the equality of the affine
Hecke parameters to finite-field equalities for the parameters attached to
rank-one Harish–Chandra inductions. In the notation of the present manuscript,
this is exactly the kind of equality supplied by
Theorem
14.1
: if
\tau
is a cuspidal
character in a Lusztig series and
u_{\tau}
is its unipotent Jordan correspondent,
then

q\!\left(R_{L}^{G}(\tau)\right)=q\!\left(R_{H_{L}}^{H}(u_{\tau})\right).

When the finite group arising from a parahoric quotient is the full quotient

\overline{G}_{x}=G(F)_{x}/G(F)_{x,0+}
, rather than only its identity component,
Lemmas
15.1
and

15.2
show that the hypotheses of the
enriched disconnected theorem, Theorem
13.4
, are satisfied.
Thus the finite Jordan-decomposition label used in the finite-field parameter
comparison is fixed by the chosen pinning
\mathcal{P}
, with the source Clifford class
retained as part of the enriched label whenever the full quotient is disconnected.
This removes the non-canonical finite Jordan-decomposition choice from the affine
parameter comparison.

This proves the claimed pinned affine-factor isomorphism (
15.4
).
Combining (
15.4
) with the source-side AFMO–Morris presentation
(
15.2
) gives

\mathcal{H}_{\mathfrak{s}}(G)\simeq\mathbb{C}[\Omega(\rho_{M}),\mu]\rtimes_{\Psi_{\mathcal{P}}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}\!\bigl(W(u_{\rho_{M}})_{\mathrm{aff}},q_{\theta}\bigr).

Here
\rtimes_{\Psi_{\mathcal{P}}}
means that the original action of

\Omega(\rho_{M})
on

\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}(W(\rho_{M})_{\mathrm{aff}},q)
is transported to the identified affine
factor by
\Psi_{\mathcal{P}}
. The twisted group algebra is still

\mathbb{C}[\Omega(\rho_{M}),\mu]
. No comparison with

\mathbb{C}[\Omega(u_{\rho_{M}}),\mu_{\theta}]
is being asserted.

Finally, the compatibility with anti-involutions follows on the affine factors
because
\Psi_{\mathcal{P}}
matches the standard generators attached to corresponding
simple reflections, and the structural AFMO–Morris isomorphism
(
15.2
) preserves the standard anti-involution by

[
24
, Theorem 4.2.2]
. This proves the theorem.
∎

Remark 15.5

(No full unipotent Hecke algebra isomorphism)
.

The theorem above is intentionally weaker than the statement it replaces. In
general one should not identify

\mathbb{C}[\Omega(\rho_{M}),\mu]\rtimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}\!\bigl(W(\rho_{M})_{\mathrm{aff}},q\bigr)

with

\mathbb{C}[\Omega(u_{\rho_{M}}),\mu_{\theta}]\rtimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}\!\bigl(W(u_{\rho_{M}})_{\mathrm{aff}},q_{\theta}\bigr).

Ohara’s theorem identifies the second, affine Hecke algebra factors. It does not
identify the finite stabilizer groups
\Omega(\rho_{M})
,
\Omega(u_{\rho_{M}})
,
nor the cocycles
\mu
,
\mu_{\theta}
. Therefore the correct consequence is the
source-side crossed product with the affine factor rewritten in unipotent terms,
not an isomorphism with the full Hecke algebra of the unipotent type. If in a
special situation one has an additional compatible identification of the finite
stabilizer groups and cocycle classes, then the affine-factor comparison may
upgrade to a full crossed-product comparison; such an additional hypothesis is
not part of the present theorem.

Remark 15.6
.

One may view Theorem
15.4
as a canonical-up-to-pinning
refinement of the affine-Hecke-algebra comparison of
[
24
, Theorem 4.4.1]
.
The finite-field ingredient in
[
24
]
uses Jordan decomposition in
Lusztig series. Here that input is replaced, for the finite quotients relevant
to the depth-zero construction, by the pinned canonical bijection of the present
paper, and by the enriched pinned bijection of Theorem
13.4

when the full quotient is disconnected.

Acknowledgements

The authors are grateful to Maarten Solleveld and Amoru Fujii for their
careful reading of earlier versions of this paper. Their questions,
comments, and corrections pointed out several errors and gaps in the
arguments, and led to significant improvements in the formulation and
proofs of the results presented here.
